<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/chutes-ladders-2026-hires-departures-firings-retirements-ceo-executives-healthcare" hreflang="en">Chutes &amp; Ladders—UHC names individual MA leader; HATCo CEO steps down</a>

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Chutes & Ladders—UHC names individual MA leader; HATCo CEO steps down" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Welcome to this week's Chutes & Ladders, our roundup of hirings, firings and retirings throughout the industry. Please submit the good news—or the bad—from your shop, and we will feature it here each week.

> UnitedHealthcare has named Suha Assi as CEO of individual Medicare Advantage, per a LinkedIn post.

Assi previously spent nearly a decade as executive director for Medicare and Retirement in Tennessee, and she previously held roles based in the state for Cigna. She wrote in the post that the "relationships with our teams, providers and communities have shaped how I think about this business and, most importantly, the people we serve."

"As I step into this national role, I’m excited about what’s ahead for Medicare Advantage," she wrote. "I look forward to working alongside teams and provider partners across the country to modernize how we work together, strengthen relationships, and find new ways to deliver for our members."

> Daryl Tol, president and CEO of General Catalyst's Health Assurance Transformation Company (HATCo) is stepping down from the role, the venture capital firm's CEO Hemant Taneja shared Tuesday.

Tol had been with the firm for about four and a half years in the wake of his time as president and CEO of AdventHealth's Central Florida Division. During that time he headed HATCo's partner and advisory network—helping grow it to nearly 40 providers, payers and other portfolio companies—and took the company's top spot partway through 2025. Of note, he helped HATCo close its acquisition of regional health system Summa Health and served as its interim CEO for much of the past year until the appointment of Jennifer Eslinger last month.

Tol is being succeeded by Reva Nohria, a founding member of the HATCo team who has been with General Catalyst since 2018. Taneja, in his announcement, said her work at both organizations has encompassed investment, company creation and strategic partnership, and that she will continue as an investor at the venture capital firm alongside her new appointment.

Tol will be supporting a smooth leadership transition and stay on in an advisory role, a representative of General Catalyst told Fierce Healthcare.

> Jennifer Shuford, M.D., is leaving her post as deputy director and chief medical officer of the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention on Nov. 12, just four months after joining, according to a Washington Post report that was confirmed by the agency on Tuesday.

The agency told press in a statement that Shuford was departing "to spend more time with family." Her appointment was announced by President Donald Trump in April, and she joined in June.

Shuford, an infectious disease specialist came to the federal stage from a role as commissioner of the Texas Department of State Health Services, and before that served as the state's chief epidemiologist. Notably, she had been credited for heading Texas' response to a measles outbreak last year. Her departure comes as the CDC clashes with state health departments and other public health leaders over its response to a growing national measles outbreak.

Satish Pillai, M.D., is slated to fill the role in the interim as the agency seeks a replacement, the CDC said.

> Johns Hopkins Health Sysem CEO Kevin Sowers is set to retire at the end of 2027.

Sowers has served as president and CEO of the Johns Hopkins system since 2018. He also previously spent more than 32 years as a leader within the Duke University Health System, including eight as CEO of Duke University Hospital.

The early announcement of his retirement plans gives Johns Hopkins significant time to conduct a search for a successor, with Theodore DeWeese, dean of the medical faculty and CEO of Johns Hopkins Medicine, leading the charge. Sowers will become a strategic adviser to Johns Hopkins to support the transition, the health system said.

"When I joined Johns Hopkins Medicine in 2018, I made a commitment to the organization and to myself to give 10 years of everything I had to this extraordinary place—to the talented, dedicated people who work here, and the people and communities we serve. And I intend to keep that commitment," Sowers said in a video message.

> Calley Means, a prominent senior advisory to Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. closely aligned with the Make America Healthy Again Movement, resigned from his role on Thursday.

The resignation was reported by the Wall Street Journal and confirmed by Means on social media.

Media reports from the past several months described Means' frustrations seeing MAHA policy priorities delayed or blunted by others in the administration, with the journal citing sources describing him as "disillusioned." Still, his time advising the secretary notched several wins for the movement including overhauled dietary guidelines, steps toward greater food ingredient transparency and loosened restrictions on psychedelics as potential medical treatments.

Means wrote in his confirmation post that he still holds "tremendous respect for President Trump and Bobby Kennedy" in their battles against "the entrenched, dark forces in D.C. that profit from Americans getting sick."

"More policy and cultural change has happened around these goals in the past two years than any time in history—and I know this movement is just getting started," he wrote.

> Premier Health, a five-hospital nonprofit academic system in Southwest Ohio, has appointed Rosanna Morris as its upcoming president and CEO, effective in the first quarter of 2027.

Morris is currently executive vice president and chief clinical operating officer at the University of Texas MD Anderson Cancer Center, and was previously president of Beaumont Hospital-Royal Oak. She is a registered nurse by training and has more than 27 years of health leadership.

She takes over for the retiring Michael Riordan, who has led Premier Health for about half a decade. During that period, he's credited, by the system's board, with stabilizing the system's finances.

> Centivo has added two leaders to its C-suite, the health plan announced.

Lisa Bielamowicz, M.D., will join the company as chief health officer, while James Parr will step into the role of chief commercial officer. Both are veteran healthcare leaders, Centivo said.

Bielamowicz has a long career as a physician and health system consultant, and will take on the chief health officer title after advising Centivo's board of directors.

Parr has previously served as a senior adviser to employers at Lockton as well as senior vice president and head of sales at Collective Health. Parr has also held leadership roles at Cigna, per the announcement.

"There are few leaders in healthcare who have the health benefits strategy knowledge and have built the strong relationships that Lisa and James bring to Centivo," said Ashok Subramanian, co-founder and CEO of Centivo, in the announcement. "These new leaders put Centivo in a strong position as we launch in new markets and help more employers across the country tackle rising healthcare costs and restore affordability by rethinking their healthcare benefits strategy."

> Inspira Health CEO Amy Mansue will be retiring at the end of the year, and will be succeeded by current President and Chief Operating Officer Warren Moore, the four-hospital South Jersey system announced this week.

Mansue will have been at the helm for six years, and is credited by the organization for expanding local care access and championing a culture of wellness among its 7,800-person workforce.

Moore has led Inspira's operations since 2021 and was promoted to president at the top of this year. He has 35 years of industry experience and will most immediately prioritize the opening of a new hospital tower and building upon the system's recent Epic launch.

“Leading Inspira has been one of the great privileges of my career,” Mansue said in the announcement. “I’m proud of what our team has accomplished and grateful for the trust of our communities. After working alongside Warren for many years, I know his ability to bring people together, navigate complex challenges and understand what our patients and neighbors count on us to deliver. I have every confidence in his leadership of Inspira’s next chapter.”

> Joint Commission has named Ken Grubbs as its chief operating officer, effective Jan. 1.

Grubbs is the first nurse to serve as COO of Joint Commission Enterprise in organization’s history, according to the Oct. 13 announcement. He currently serves as the organization's chief nursing executive and executive vice president of Accreditation and Certification Operations.

He will succeed James Merlino, M.D., who will take over as the organization's president and CEO this coming January.

> Beth Israel Lahey Health's national leadership search has yielded Peter Shorett as its next president and CEO. His appointment takes effect this coming January.

Shorett is currently the system's senior executive vice president and chief operating officer, and played a role bringing together its Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center and Dana-Farber Cancer Institute for an upcoming strategic collaboration for cancer care. Prior to joining in 2019, he was a senior partner at Chartis.

He'll be taking over for Kevin Tabb, M.D., who in March gave notice to the system's board that he plans to step down after this year. He'd the role at Beth Israel Deaconess since 2011 and guided the organization through its 2019 merger with Lahey Health.

> WellSpan's Board of Directors has named Laureen Driscoll as the Pennsylvania-based system's new president and CEO.

The executive and registered nurse's appointment will go into effect Jan. 4, 2027. She takes over for Roxanna Gapstur, Ph.D., who announced plans to retire this past spring.

Driscoll is joining the 12-hospital, $4.7 billion nonprofit from Providence, where she currently serves as chief executive for its south division and oversees 17 hospitals with $10.3 billion of net operating revenue. Prior to that she held roles at MultiCare Health System, Swedish Health System, Le Bonheur Children's Medical Center and Maine Medical Center.

“She has led complex health systems through transformation while strengthening quality, culture, access and performance," Board Chair Joe Crosswhite said in the announcement. "Just as important, she leads with purpose and understands that listening to team members, clinicians and communities is essential. The Board is confident Laureen is the right leader for WellSpan’s next chapter.”

> UnitedHealthcare has elevated Bobby Hunter to the role of president at the insurer.

Hunter wrote in a LinkedIn post that in the new position he will be tasked with leading the charge on the company's modernization efforts, including investments in technology and other initiatives to simplify the healthcare experience for its members.

Hunter most recently served as CEO of Government Programs at UnitedHealthcare, and has held multiple leadership roles at the insurer dating back to 2011.

"The future we're working toward is one where plans and providers work together more seamlessly, information flows instantly, trust is earned through transparency and accountability, and care teams are able to spend more time focused on patients," he wrote in the post. "To get there, we must continue to deliver on the commitments we make to the people, providers, customers, and communities who count on us every day."

> Jonathan B. Perlin, M.D., Ph.D., will retire from his roles as president and CEO of accrediting organization Joint Commission on Dec. 31. Current Chief Operating Officer James I. Merlino, M.D., is slated to succeed him.

Perlin boasted three decades of healthcare executive leadership across his career, which included five at the top of Joint Commission. During that period he oversaw a substantial reduction on Joint Commission standards deemed to be burdensome or obsolete, and programs to support healthcare safety issues in low- and middle-income countries. Titles preceding his time at the organization included president of clinical operations and chief medical officer for HCA Healthcare, and the U.S. Department of Veteran's Affairs' under secretary for health.

Perlin will remain a strategic advisor to the group following his retirement, holding the title of president emeritus.

Merlino's career included leadership roles at Cleveland Clinic such as chief clinical transformation officer and chief experience officer, as well as at healthcare performance improvement company Press Ganey.

> Robert C. Garrett, CEO of Hackensack Meridian Health, will retire next year after decades with the organization, including its formative 2016 merger.

Garrett is set to step down on June 30, upon which time he'll hold the role of CEO emeritus for a year to help ease the transition. The 18-hospital, nearly $10 billion nonprofit said it plans to announce his successor before the year's end.

Garrett began his career in the early 1980s at Hackensack University Medical Center, and in 2009 was named president and CEO of Hackensack University Health Network. When the organization merged with Meridian Health in 2016 to form New Jersey's largest health system, he served as co-CEO for two years before taking on the solo mantle.

Alongside the organization's growth to more than 500 patient care locations, Garrett's tenure also marked the opening of the Hackensack Meridian School of Medicine, New Jersey's first new medical school in decades.

"Bob has been the driving force behind HMH's unprecedented growth and transformation, its superior quality, innovation and commitment to building an exceptional workforce," Board Chair Keith Banks said in a statement. "At the heart of Bob's legacy is his unwavering belief that we must deliver the most innovative and compassionate care to all patients. HMH is solidly positioned for continued growth, and we are profoundly grateful to Bob and extend our warmest wishes to him for this well-deserved next chapter."

> The Pharmaceutical Care Management Association, the leading lobbying group for the pharmacy benefit management industry, has named CVS Health executive Ed DeVaney as its board chair.

DeVaney currently serves as president of CVS Caremark, one of the PBM industry's "Big Three" firms. He succeeds Adam Kautzner, president of Express Scripts and Evernorth Health Services, representing one of the other "Big Three" companies.

“The role PBMs play in lowering costs and improving access to prescription drugs for Americans is critically important,” DeVaney said in the announcement. “As Board Chair, I’m committed to ensuring PCMA shares our story with policymakers, consumers and employers so they have a full understanding of the work we do to fulfill our commitment to lower drug costs.”

> UnitedHealth Group has elevated Dan Kueter to serve as the company's chief commercial officer, per a LinkedIn post.

Kueter previously served as the CEO of UnitedHealthcare's commercial business for four years. UHC is the health insurance arm of UnitedHealth Group.

In the new role, Kueter will be tasked with working across both UnitedHealthcare and Optum to support coordination in product development and innovation, he wrote in the post. He said he will also collaborate closely with other senior leaders at the UnitedHealth Group level.

"I'm looking forward to working across the company with colleagues at Optum and UnitedHealthcare to help drive our next chapter of growth and innovation, focused on helping make healthcare work better for everyone," Kueter wrote.

In a separate post, Brandon Cuevas revealed that he will succeed Kueter as CEO of UHC's commercial unit. Cuevas most recently served as executive vice president for the national health plan at Kaiser Permanente, and he previously held multiple leadership roles during a 14-year stint at UnitedHealthcare.

Cuevas said in the post that he "couldn’t be more excited to rejoin a team that is so capable and committed to making positive change in healthcare."

> Optum has named Ujjwal Ratan as the new chief AI officer for its Optum Insight unit, per a LinkedIn post.

In the role, he will be tasked with leading the development of the critical infrastructure that Optum Insight will build its agentic AI toolset on. Ratan has previously held leadership roles at Amazon Web Services and at Oracle, according to his profile.

"Agentic AI only works if the agents have real, connected data to reason over — and few organizations sit across as much of the healthcare journey as Optum does: providers, payers, pharmacy, life sciences and government programs, under one roof," Ratan wrote. "That's exactly the foundation this next wave of AI needs, and exactly why I think Optum is positioned to set the standard for agentic AI across healthcare, not just inside it."

> Association of American Medical Colleges President and CEO David J. Skorton, M.D., will retire from his post on June 30, 2027, the group announced this week. A search for his replacement is currently under way.

Skorton stepped into the role in July 2019 and was kept on by the board in a unanimous 2023 vote. He's credited with guiding the association through the pandemic and overseeing a comprehensive strategic planning process that brought a new vision and mission. Prior to joining he served as secretary of the Smithsonian Institution and was president of Cornell University and the University of Iowa.

“He will be leaving the AAMC an even stronger association than when he arrived," Board Chair Michael Waldrum, M.D., said in a statement. "Identifying a visionary leader to guide the association and the academic medicine community into the future will be our next challenge.”

> Cotiviti, a healthcare infrastructure and data analytics platform, has appointed Erin Schmuker as its chief operating officer.

She enters the role with over 20 years of healthcare leadership, coming directly from a job as president of integrated pharmacies at OptumRx. The new position tasks Schumuker with scaling Cotiviti's operating model, bolstering execution and accountability, simplifying operations and bolstering its growth.

“Our opportunity is to connect data and intelligence to the workflows where decisions are actually made and turn that intelligence into action at scale," President and CEO Ric Sinclair said of the appointment. "Erin is a proven operator who understands how to do that. She knows healthcare, she knows our clients and she knows how to bring people, technology and operations together to deliver results. She will help us move faster, operate better and scale Cotiviti for what healthcare needs next.”

> Kaiser Permanente recently announced that Shaun Smith will be joining as executive vice president and chief people officer, effective Oct. 12.

Smith, who will be reporting directly to the CEO as well as to the president of integrated care and coverage, comes from a chief people and culture officer roles at NewYork-Presbyterian.

> The Blue Cross Blue Shield Association (BCBSA) has named Dan Serrano senior vice president and chief financial officer to oversee the organization’s enterprise financial strategy.

Serrano joins BCBSA after holding leadership roles across the health insurance, provider and value-based care sectors. His experience includes serving as chief financial officer for Aetna's Mid-Atlantic Region and holding senior finance positions at ChenMed, Healthfirst, as well as several other home-based and primary care organizations. He also served as vice president of value and risk-based contracting at Mount Sinai Health System.

“Dan's extensive experience across the healthcare ecosystem, combined with a strong track record of aligning financial performance to operational outcomes, makes him a tremendous addition to our leadership team,” said BCBSA president and CEO Kim Keck in a statement.

>Keck Medicine of USC announced that CEO Rodney Hanners will be stepping down on Sept. 24, alongside a handful of other leadership changes at the system and its affiliated Keck School of Medicine of USC.

Hanners was in the role for six years, a period in which he extended the Los Angeles-based organization's reach into Pasadena and the San Gabriel Valley.

Taking over for him will be Steven Shapiro, M.D., currently senior vice president for health affairs, who joined in 2021 from a run as president of the University of Pittsburg Medical Center.

The university noted that it will be trimming the role's responsibilities, reverting supervision of some USC health science schools to the provost "to allow Steve to focus more intensely on elevating our academic medical enterprise."

Beneath him, the university has also named Chief Clinical Officer David Peng, M.D., as deputy executive vice president for health affairs and Keck Medical Center of USC CEO Marty Sargeant as chief operating officer of the broader system. Both Peng and Sargeant will remain in their prior roles alongside the new responsibilities.

USC's announcement also included word that Keck School of Medicine of USC Dean Carolyn Meltzer is stepping down for a new role as president of the Radiological Society of North America. Vassilios Papadopoulos, dean of the university's pharmaceutical school, will be filling her role on an interim basis.

>Health tech company Health Catalyst appointed Simeon Kohl as Chief Executive Officer and President and a member of the company’s Board of Directors, effective September 14.

Kohl most recently served as CEO of Performant Healthcare, a public healthcare company he led through significant strategic and operational transformation, culminating in its sale to Machinify for approximately $670 million. He brings more than 20 years in healthcare, spanning operations, growth and public company leadership. The company’s current CEO and President, Ben Albert, will become Chief Business Officer and will step down from the Board of Directors effective September 13.

During his tenure, Albert led Health Catalyst's strategic priorities and operating initiatives and aligned the leadership team now running the business. He sharpened the company’s focus on its highest-conviction technologies, positioning Health Catalyst as an AI-forward healthcare intelligence company, executives said. To support that focus, he led the divestiture of Vitalware, which enabled the company to retire its approximately $160 million credit facility and strengthen its balance sheet.

> The American Medical Association tapped Jennifer Goldsack as senior vice president and inaugural leader of the AMA Center for Digital Health and AI. Goldsack joins the AMA from the Digital Medicine Society (DiMe), a global non-profit organization dedicated to advancing digital medicine to optimize health, where she was both founder and CEO.

A leading voice on the responsible adoption of digital health and AI in healthcare, Goldsack has a record of bringing together evidence, clinical practice, technology and policy to help ensure digital innovations are safe, effective, equitable and capable of delivering meaningful value for patients and physicians, according to the AMA.

"We are at a defining moment for medicine. Digital health and augmented intelligence are rapidly and fundamentally changing how and where care is delivered. For these tools to reach their full potential and be effective for patients, physicians must lead the way in developing and implementing them. Jennifer is the right leader for this moment because she knows how to bring physicians and innovators together and translate that collaboration into real-world impact on patient care," AMA CEO John Whyte, M.D., said.

“Reimagining how we care for people in the digital era has been the focus of my career, and we are at an extraordinary moment of opportunity,” Goldsack said in a statement. “We have powerful new tools at our disposal and a responsibility to make sure we harness them to improve people’s lives. I’m thrilled to join the AMA and work alongside physicians and partners across healthcare. We’re going to set a high bar for what better healthcare looks like in the digital and AI era. And together, we will make it happen.”

Since its launch last year, the AMA Center for Digital Health and AI has produced key research, including: a survey of AI usage among physicians, showing growing confidence in the technology’s clinical advantages; and an international survey on wearables.

>Teladoc Health appointed Michael Grasher as its new chief financial officer.

Grasher has more than three decades of experience across the insurance and financial services industries, most recently serving as CFO of property-casualty insurance company IFG Companies, according to an Aug. 31 press release.

"Teladoc Health has built a strong foundation with unmatched scale, deep clinical expertise and a global footprint," said Grasher. "I'm excited to work with Chuck [Divita] and the leadership team to build on that foundation and deliver lasting value for the members, clients and shareholders we serve."

>Johns Hopkins Health Plan has named Daniel Chojnowski as its interim CEO.

Chojnowski has served as the insurer's chief financial officer for the past seven years, according to a post from JHHP on LinkedIn. Former JHHP CEO James Holland departed the organization in mid-August to become president of Medicaid for Humana.

JHHP said in the LinkedIn posted that Chojnowski will "provide stability and trusted guidance" while it conducts a search for a permanent CEO.

"Dan believes in leading with courage and compassion," per the post. "He embodies the modern CFO, which has evolved from the number-crunching budget guard to a strategic partner, helping to steer organizational decisions."

>Wilmington, Delaware-based ChristianaCare welcomed Jenn Schwartz as its new president and CEO on Sept. 1.

The new top exec came to came to ChristianaCare in 2018 to initially serve as chief legal officer, having previously spent over a decade at Lourdes Health System where she was vice president of legal affairs and general counsel as well as its executive director. She'd already been named as executive vice president and chief strategy officer earlier this year.

Schwartz is taking over for Janice Nevin, M.D., who announced earlier this year she'd be retiring after about 23 years at the nonprofit system. She'd held the top roles for a dozen years.

ChristianaCare is a nonprofit teaching health system with three hospitals hosting 1,430 beds as well as a network of primary care and outpatient services.

Janice Nevin, M.D., will end her 12-year run as president and CEO of ChristianaCare on Sept. 1, the system's board announced.

She retires after 23 years at the organization, initially joining as its chair of family and community medicine. The physician executive is credited by the board with expanding the system's physical footprint, shepherding "significant investment in historically under-resourced areas" and strengthening its academic partnerships.

Taking up the mantle will be Jenn Schwartz, who was named executive vice president and chief strategy officer at the top of 2026. She came to ChristianaCare in 2018 to initially serve as chief legal officer. Prior to that she spent over a decade at Lourdes Health System, where she was vice president of legal affairs & general counsel as well as its executive director.

>Insurer Centene has named Bradley Bolivar as its new chief information officer, with the new role taking effect on Aug. 31.

Bolivar succeeds Brian LeClare, who is set to retire in October. He previously served as CIO at Fannie Mae, where he led strategy around technological development, infrastructure, cybersecurity and more. Bolivar brings more than three decades of technology leadership experience to the role at Centene, according to an announcement from the company.

"We are entering a new era where data, technology and AI are not just business enablers, but strategic capabilities that shape how we operate, innovate and create value," CEO Sarah London said in the announcement. "Brad's proven ability to solve complex technology challenges will position Centene to deliver simpler, better health experiences and improved outcomes for the nation's most underserved populations."

>Elevance Health has named Patrick Fox, M.D., as president of Carelon Behavioral Health.

Fox's background includes more than three decades of experience in clinical psychiatry, behavioral health, public health and health plan leadership, the company said in an announcement. He most recently served as president Elevance-affiliated plan Wellpoint New Jersey.

Fox also previously helmed the company's Colorado Medicaid plan, per the announcement. Fox's new title is effective immediately.

"Patrick brings a rare combination of experience as a practicing psychiatrist, behavioral health leader and health plan executive,” said Kristy Duffey, president of Carelon Health, in the announcement. “He understands the challenges facing behavioral healthcare from the clinical, operational and system perspectives."

"That breadth of experience will be invaluable as we work to improve access, strengthen connections across the healthcare system and deliver better outcomes for the people we serve," said Duffey.

Patrick Gillespie, regional vice president for state affairs at Elevance Health, has been named as Fox's successor as Wellpoint New Jersey president, the company said.

>Providence St. Joseph Health has named a new chief financial officer.

Kevin Smith will take the CFO seat on Oct. 26, according to an announcement from the health system. As CFO, he'll oversee Providence's "inancial strategy, planning, capital allocation and operational performance," the system said.

Smith will join Providence from SSM Health, where he currently serves as CFO. He's also held leadership roles in finance at Luminis Health, Lehigh Valley Health Network, Universal Health Services and the University of Pennsylvania Health System.

“Kevin is a highly respected healthcare finance leader with a strong track record of improving performance, guiding complex organizations and positioning health systems for long-term success,” said Erik Wexler, CEO of Providence, in the announcement. “His experience, judgment and commitment to Mission-driven stewardship will be invaluable as we continue strengthening Providence for the future.”

>Accolade executive Shantanu Nundy will join Humana as its new chief medical officer.

Nundy, M.D., currently serves as executive vice president of care delivery and chief health officer at Accolade, a healthcare navigation and care delivery company. He'll step into the role at Humana on Aug. 31, according to an announcement from the company.

Nundy also held leadership roles at Evolent Health, and has provided council to a number of organizations on digital health and AI, including the Food and Drug Administration and World Bank. He continues to practice medicine, including primary care a community health center and hospital-based medicine through a local health system.

Nundy will report to Humana CEO Jim Rechtin as part of the enterprise leadership team.

"Clinical excellence is at the heart of Humana’s vision to be a leading consumer healthcare company, and I am thrilled to welcome Dr. Nundy as our new chief medical officer," Rechtin said in the announcement. "I am confident Shantanu’s experience at the intersection of clinical outcomes, technology and the consumer experience will advance our efforts to simplify the path to care and help the people we serve achieve their best health."

>St. Jude Children's Research Hospital has named Charles W.M. Roberts, M.D., Ph.D., as its next president and CEO, effective Jan. 1, 2027.

He currently serves as director of the St. Jude Comprehensive Cancer Center, having joined the organization in 2015 to fill the post. The pediatric hematologist/oncologist has headed the center's five research programs, pushed the center to its best scores in National Cancer Institute rankings and has his own body of medical research that has paved the way to FDA-approved and investigational cancer therapies.

His appointment follows the February announcement from St. Jude that its current leader, James R. Downing, M.D., planned to step down. The organization said it conducted "an extensive international search" before selecting a successor from within.

>CVS Health has announced changes to its board of directors, including the addition of JPMorgan Chase executive Teresa Heitsenrether.

Heitsenrether brings nearly forty years of experience in financial leadership to the board, and currently serves as the chief data and analytics officer at JPMorgan. She will join CVS' board on Nov. 18.

“Teresa’s extensive experience leading complex global businesses and driving technology-enabled transformation will be invaluable as CVS Health continues to innovate on its path to become a consumer focused, health technology company,” CVS CEO David Joyner said in the announcement.

In addition, Larry Robbins has departed CVS' board as of Aug. 13. Robbins has served on the board since 2024, with roles on its Audit and Public Policy and External Affairs Committees.

Robbins is the CEO of Glenview Capital Management.

>Drew Asher, chief financial officer at Centene, will step down from the position in December, the company announced on Monday.

Asher intends to retire from Centene at the end of 2027, per the announcement. He will take the year between stepping down as CFO and retirement to support the transition and ongoing strategic priorities.

Chris Neczypor has been tapped as Asher's successor and will join the company in September, according to the release. Neczypor is a veteran executive who most recently served as CFO at Lincoln Financial, which provides life insurance, annuities and retirement solutions.

He had multiple leadership roles at Lincoln after joining the company in 2018, Centene said. Neczypor spent more than a decade in financial services prior to Lincoln, including a stint as an equity research analyst at Goldman Sachs.

"Chris brings a proven track record of strengthening financial performance, optimizing operations and creating long-term shareholder value," Centene CEO Sarah London said in the announcement. "Just as importantly, he is a collaborative, values-driven leader who understands how to build strong teams and drive meaningful impact."

"Our organization will benefit from his expertise, energy and perspective as we deliver on Centene's next phase of transformation and growth," London said.

Asher has served as CFO at Centene since 2021.

>Humana has named a new executive to lead its growing Medicaid business.

The insurer said Monday that James Holland will join the company as senior vice president and president of Medicaid effective Aug. 17. In the role, he will oversee plans that cover 1.6 million people across 11 states, according to an announcement.

Holland most recently helmed Johns Hopkins Health Plans, overseeing Medicaid, Medicare and other government plans. He has also held leadership roles at Elevance Health, Amerigroup and WellCare Health Plans.

Holland will report to Aaron Martin, president of insurance at Humana.

“Medicaid is an important part of how we deliver on Humana’s purpose, helping some of the most vulnerable populations access the care and support they need to achieve their best health,” Martin said in the announcement. “J.P. brings deep expertise leading government-sponsored healthcare plans and maintaining strong relationships with state and provider partners."

"His experiences and insights will be invaluable as we continue to grow our Medicaid business and better serve our Medicaid members," Martin said.

>Amid the health system's financial strengthening project, Tufts Medicine CEO and CFO have transitioned away from the company.

The current CEO, Mike Dandorph, will step down on September 30, which is also the conclusion of the 2026 fiscal year. He will continue to work towards a transition that ensures Tufts Medicine continues its upward trajectory.

Phil Okala, the COO of Tufts Medicine, will serve as Acting President and CEO. Phil has held multiple leadership roles at Tufts Medicine, and other health systems, including Penn Medicine. With the Board’s full support, he hopes to “focus on guiding Tufts Medicine to greater clinical, operational and financial success.”

Andrew DeVoe, the current Executive Vice President and CFO, has also decided to depart Tufts Medicine, after playing an important role in improving the organization's performance. Soon, Tufts Medicine expects to begin a nationwide search for his replacement. As for right now, Greg Kruse, Tufts’ Vice President of Strategic Operations and Analytics Intelligence, will assume responsibility for future budget planning.

Dandorph said “Serving as President and CEO of Tufts Medicine and working closely with such extraordinary people has been a distinct privilege these past six and a half years. I am proud of what we have accomplished together and am grateful to the amazing physicians, nurses, researchers, learners, staff and leaders whose commitment to our mission has inspired me every day.  I believe this transition is in the best interest and right time for Tufts Medicine. In the coming months, I will work closely with Phil Okala to ensure a thoughtful and orderly transition that best positions the organization for its next chapter and future success.”

>Summa Health announced on August 3 that Jennifer Eslinger will join as the health system's new President and CEO beginning on September 21. She will succeed Daryl Tol, the current Acting CEO, who has led the organization following its acquisition by Health Assurance Transformation Company (HATCo).

Eslinger brings more than two decades of leadership experience; most recently serving as President and COO of Rochester Regional Health. At Rochester Regional Health, she led the $4.2 billion health system through significant transformation and strategic growth.

"Jennifer is an accomplished healthcare leader with deep experience driving operational and clinical excellence, embracing innovation and creating a culture of curiosity and continuous improvement,” said Kate Walsh, Chair of Summa Health’s Board of Directors. “She has both the judgment and values to build on Summa Health's strong foundation while accelerating our impact in the communities we serve and helping shape the future of healthcare.”

>Jaewon Ryu, the chief executive officer of Risant Health at Kaiser Permanente, has decided to step down from the role to focus on his family. Dale Maxwell, former president and CEO of Presbyterian Healthcare Services, will serve as interim CEO during the national search by the board.

"Jaewon has been instrumental in turning the vision for Risant Health into reality," said Greg Adams, chair and chief executive officer of Kaiser Permanente and chair of the board of Risant Health. "He built an exceptional leadership team, established the organization's operating model, and positioned Risant Health to help more communities benefit from value-based care.”

Ryu served as Risant Health’s inaugural CEO, establishing its operating model and recruiting the initial leadership team. Key moments in his tenure include the addition of both Geisinger and Cone Health as well as meaningful clinical and operational improvements.

The interim CEO, Dale Maxwell, brings over 24 years of executive leadership experience at Presbyterian Healthcare Services, New Mexico's largest nonprofit integrated health system.

"This leadership transition reflects thoughtful planning, not a change in strategy," Adams said. "Kaiser Permanente and Risant Health will continue to advance their mission to help leading community-based health systems deliver high-quality, value-based care. We are confident in the organization's leadership, its momentum, and its future."

>Paul Smith and Frederick Crawford were elected as new members of the Humana board, effective immediately.

Smith currently serves as the chief commercial officer at Anthropic, PBC, leading commercial strategy and go-to-market operations. Prior to joining Anthropic, he was chief commercial officer and president of global customer and field operations at ServiceNow and held senior leadership roles at Salesforce and Microsoft.

Crawford also brings significant experience to the Humana board, having spent more than 30 years in the insurance and banking industries. Before his retirement in 2024, Crawford was president and chief operating officer at Aflac. He also sits on the Board of Webster Financial Corporation as a member of both the Audit and Risk committees.

“We are pleased to welcome Paul and Fred to the Humana Board. Paul has deep experience guiding the world’s largest enterprises in responsibly adopting technology and AI at scale along with a strong track record in product development and client service, while Fred brings broad industry experience and financial expertise focused on disciplined capital allocation and risk management,” said Humana Chairman of the Board Kurt J. Hilzinger.

>Centene announced that Kenneth A. Burdick has retired from the company's board of directors, effective July 28, and that Paul J. Diaz has been appointed to the board, effective immediately.

Burdick joined the Centene board of directors in January 2022, offering insight that drew upon his long-standing leadership in healthcare. He served as executive vice president of products and markets at Centene and before that was the chief executive officer of WellCare Health Plans.

Diaz is currently a managing partner at Cressey & Company, a private investment firm focused on cultivating high-quality healthcare firms. Before joining Cressey, he was president and CEO of Myriad Genetics Inc., as well as CEO of Kindred Healthcare. He has also served on the board of healthcare organizations, such as DaVita and PharMerica.

"Ken has been a valued member of our Board. His deep industry expertise, strategic perspective, and commitment to improving healthcare have made a meaningful impact on Centene. On behalf of the Board, we thank him for his service and wish him all the best in retirement," said Frederick H. Eppinger, Chair of Centene's Board of Directors. "We are also pleased to welcome Paul Diaz to the Board. His extensive healthcare leadership experience, public company governance expertise and record of guiding organizations through growth and transformation will bring valuable perspective as Centene continues to advance its strategy and serve its members."

>Siemens Healthineers announced on July 30 that João Seabra will succeed John Kowal as president and head of the Americas for Siemens Healthineers, effective Oct. 1, 2026. He is currently the head of Siemens Healthineers Enterprise Services.

Seabra joined Healthineers in 2001, and from 2015 to 2018, he led the Western Europe and West Africa regions. In his most recent role as head of enterprise services, he quadrupled its revenue over the last eight years.

“We would like to thank John for his many years of successful service and outstanding commitment to Siemens Healthineers, first within our Varian business and later as President and Head of our Americas business. We are very pleased to appoint João Seabra as our new President and Head of the Americas. Under his leadership and thanks to his innovative thinking, Siemens Healthineers paved the way in our industry by creating Value Partnerships of increasing scope and impact with key customers,” said Darleen Caron, Member of the Managing Board at Siemens Healthineers with responsibility for the Americas and Chief Human Resources Officer.

>PayZen, an AI-driven patient affordability platform, has announced that Jingwei Wu will serve as senior vice president of engineering.

Prior to joining PayZen, Wu led the engineering team behind the LinkedIn Jobs Marketplace, expanding it to reach millions more job searchers. He’s also been part of engineering leadership teams at both Twitter and Tinder.

In his new role at PayZen, Wu will lead the company’s engineering organization, overseeing platform architecture, AI infrastructure and product engineering.

“Healthcare affordability is one of the biggest challenges facing patients and providers today, and PayZen is proving that AI can fundamentally improve how people access and pay for care,” Wu said.

>Sarah Bast was appointed as the chief marketing officer of OptimizeRx, a healthcare technology solutions provider.

She brings over two decades of experience from companies including Publicis Health Media, Lilly, Bayer, Johnson & Johnson and AstraZeneca. In these roles, she developed significant expertise in point of care media strategy, which she will bring to her role at OptimizeRX.

As CMO, Bast will lead Optimize Rx’s marketing strategy and continue strengthening the company's relationships with healthcare marketing agencies.

"Sarah's decision to join OptimizeRx speaks to both the strength of the business we've built and the tremendous opportunity ahead," said Steve Silvestro, Chief Executive Officer, OptimizeRx. "She has spent her career helping life sciences brands solve complex marketing challenges and understands firsthand how healthcare marketers can reach providers more effectively. As OptimizeRx enters its next phase of growth, her strategic vision and deep understanding of the market will help us strengthen our customer relationships and accelerate the development of solutions that improve healthcare engagement."

>Matt Swafford has been named executive vice president and chief financial officer of Peace Health, succeeding Ric Magnuson, the current interim CFO. He will join the company on September 21 from St. Charles Health System, where he currently serves as CFO and senior VP of strategy and finance.

Swafford brings over 35 years of experience in nonprofit health system finance, helping health systems navigate change and invest in patients.

Sarah Ness, PeaceHealth president and chief executive officer, said, “We are creating a stronger PeaceHealth, one that is easier for patients to navigate, better connected for caregivers and positioned to meet the needs of our communities well into the future. Matt has shared with me that he sees every financial decision through that human lens, which makes him a strong fit for our Mission and our commitment to put people first.”

>Hannah Alphs Jackson, M.D. will join Availity, the nation’s largest real-time health information network, as the company's first chief medical officer.

Availity said in a statement that it created the CMO role “to ensure clinical expertise is injected throughout the company's strategy, innovation, and customer solutions.” This is significant as the healthcare industry and Availity continues to become dependent on automation and AI, executives said.

Before joining Availity, Alphs Jackson held roles in physician and payer leadership, where she integrated value-based care models with clinical operations. In her new role as CMO, she will guide the responsible usage of emerging technologies, as part of Availity’s clinical strategy.

“Dr. Jackson brings a rare combination of clinical expertise, provider perspective, payer experience and strategic vision,” said Russ Thomas, CEO of Availity, in a statement. “As healthcare continues to evolve, it's critical that we have strong clinical leadership guiding how we integrate automation and AI capabilities to support the entire healthcare system – and ultimately improve the entire experience.”

>Clinical AI assistant startup Nabla announced the appointment of Brian Manning as its new CEO to lead the company's next phase of growth.

Manning joins Nabla from Bamboo Health, where he served as president and chief revenue officer and helped scale the company to more than $140 million in revenue. He also assisted in the growth and development of PatientPing and Zocdoc.

As Nabla continues to expand, supporting more than 190 healthcare organizations and 100,000 clinicians, Manning will spearhead the company’s scaling efforts. His more than 20 years of experience accelerating growth will ensure that Nabla continues to be adopted across the healthcare industry, executives said in a press release.

"Healthcare organizations are looking for partners that can deliver real value today while helping them prepare for the future of AI. Nabla has built an exceptional foundation and has earned the trust of clinicians and healthcare organizations. The opportunity now is to accelerate that momentum, expand our reach, and further establish Nabla as the clinical AI partner of choice for healthcare organizations across the world,” said Manning.

Wiley’s appointment comes at a significant time for Aptarro as the company continue to scale its engine for denial prevention and reimbursement intelligence. Wiley has more than two decades of executive leadership experience spanning healthcare technology, enterprise software, AI and travel technology, according to the company, and he has been tapped to steer the company through its current commercial growth.

Most recently, he served as chief commercial officer at Avodah, and prior to that, he founded Opargo, an AI-powered healthcare scheduling company. His experience will ensure that Aptarro continues to effectively address the persistent and costly problem of denials, executives said.

"As reimbursement continues to grow more dynamic, healthcare organizations need technology that evolves alongside it. We're building the leadership team to support that mission," said Jeff Diamond, Aptarro CEO. "Paul's experience will be instrumental as we continue expanding our platform and defining the future of reimbursement intelligence."

>Vanderbilt Health has chosen David C. Miller as the successor to Jeff Balser as President and CEO and Dean of Vanderbilt University School of Medicine.

Miller is currently serving as Chief Executive Officer of Michigan Medicine and Executive Vice President for Medical Affairs for the University of Michigan and will assume the role at Vanderbilt on January 1, 2027. Earlie this year, Balser announced plans to retire on December 31, after serving Vanderbilt in leadership roles for nearly two decades.

Miller’s experience at the similarly sized Michigan Health uniquely prepares him to become Vanderbilt Health’s new leader, the health system said. Miller’s selection as the new senior leader for Vanderbilt Health and Vanderbilt University School of Medicine finalizes an extensive national search by the VH Board of Directors to identify Balser’s successor.

As Chief Executive Officer, Miller will be responsible for shaping the future strategic direction of Vanderbilt Health and maintaining the scholarly activities shared by the Medical Center and University.

“From an incredibly strong field of applicants, Dr. Miller’s personal warmth, prior leadership experiences, and commitment to organizational culture stood out. David has presented an exciting vision for advancing the Medical Center’s missions and is the ideal candidate to succeed Dr. Balser. We look forward to welcoming David, his wife, Inge, and family to the Nashville community,” said Edie Carell Johnson, Chair of the VH Board of Directors.

>Aledade appointed Darren Thayne as chief technology officer and hired Josh C. Mandel, M.D. as chief scientist. Thayne and Mandel will bring deep expertise in enterprise scale and open data standards to the company, Aledade said.

Leveraging their combined experience, Aledade will further integrate advanced AI into its platform.

Thayne joins Aledade from Domo, where he spent 15 years leading its technology team, as founding technology leader, chief technology officer and executive vice president of product. He was responsible for breakthrough technology and deployment of AI-based tools that were central to customer experience.

Dr. Mandel, who is both a physician and software engineer, joins Aledade from Microsoft research, where he helped build open standards such as FHIR (Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources). His work will focus on external interoperability and data exchange efforts, as well as AI strategy.

“AI raises the stakes on that alignment. Deployed inside fee-for-service, it accelerates billing. Deployed inside our value-based care model, it accelerates health. Daren and Josh have built infrastructure and open standards at national scale, and they're joining us to make sure that the value created by AI in healthcare flows to patients and practices,” said Farzad Mostashari, M.D., co-founder and CEO of Aledade.

>Brian S. Kim, M.D. has been named the first vice president and chief biotechnology officer at Northwell Health, as well as head of intellectual asset management at the Feinstein Institutes. He will also join the department of dermatology as a professor at Northwell Health.

The Feinstein Institutes is home to Northwell Health’s research center, and it serves as the largest health care provider and private employer in New York State. Kim joins Feinstein from the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai, bringing his expertise in dermatology, medical research and biotechnology.

His work has led to significant clinical advances, including the design of pivotal clinical trials that resulted in two first-in-class U.S. Food and Drug Administration-approved treatments for atopic dermatitis and prurigo nodularis. He has founded three biotech companies, including Alys Pharmaceuticals, and authored over 190 peer-reviewed publications.

Kim will continue to advance Feinstein Institute’s groundbreaking research on electric stimulation to treat disease, including work on nerve stimulation implants that could potentially treat Crohn’s disease and other inflammatory conditions.

“Dr. Kim’s appointment reflects our commitment to transforming bold research into better outcomes for our patients,” said John D’Angelo, M.D., Northwell’s president and CEO. “With his leadership, we can better translate breakthrough science and pioneer the future of medicine while staying grounded in our mission to produce knowledge to cure disease.”

>The University of Oklahoma’s academic health system (OU Health) has named Jonathan W. Curtright as the new president and CEO, effective July 1, 2026. Richard P. Lofgren, M.D., OU Health’s inaugural president and CEO, announced his retirement earlier this year and Curtright will be his replacement.

Curtright has been chief operating officer of OU Health since August 22, and under his leadership, the system saw an annual revenue increase from $1.9 billion to a projected $3.7 billion in fiscal year 2026. Admissions also increased 22.3% percent and outpatient clinic visits statewide reached more than 1 million, reflecting higher patient volumes and service intensity as opposed to acquisitions.

He was also responsible for program expansions, including investments in pediatric health, cancer centers and neonatal intensive care.

OU Health board of directors Chair G. Rainey Williams Jr. said, “Jonathan has been instrumental in helping advance OU Health’s mission and strategic vision during a period of extraordinary growth. He understands the unique responsibilities of an academic health system and has demonstrated the leadership, operational expertise and long-term vision needed to guide OU Health into its next chapter. The board is confident in his ability to build upon the strong foundation established under Dr. Lofgren’s leadership.”

>Christopher O’Connor, the CEO of Yale New Haven Health System (YNHHS), will be leaving after 14 years with the organization.

He will serve as a special advisor to the chair of the Board of Trustees during the transition, and the board will also be conducting a CEO search over the next months. Pamela Sutton-Wallace, the current president of YNHHS, will serve as acting CEO, effective immediately.

Wathen was initially named senior vice president and CFO of Deaconess in 2010, after serving as manager of internal audit, manager of finance, CFO of The Women's Hospital and controller of Deaconess Health System.

Klomp, who heads the Department of Medicare at the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services, has made a rapid rise through the agency to a potential role as its second-in-command. The Deputy Secretary post has been vacant since February, when Jim O'Neill departed the agency. At the same time, Klomp was elevated to a senior adviser to HHS Secretary Robert F. Kennedy, Jr.

In the Truth Social post, Trump said that Klomp's career trajectory "proves that he is a potential STAR." He said that he, Kennedy and CMS Administrator Mehmet Oz, M.D., chose Klomp as a nominee "together."

"Chris is a Strong and Inspiring Leader and a Highly Successful Entrepreneur," Trump wrote in the post. "Everywhere Chris goes, he earns TRUST. He is a person of principle, and is deeply committed to serving the AMERICAN People — and fixing our broken Healthcare System."

>Rush University System for Health and Rush University Medical Center announced today that Omar Lateef, D.O., president and CEO, intends to retire from his role in June 2027. The Rush Board of Directors also announced that it will begin a national search for the next system leader in early fall.

Lateef has been connected to Rush for over 25 years, completing his fellowship training in Pulmonary and Critical Care Medicine at Rush University Medical Center in 2005 and joining the faculty afterwards. In 2019, he became the chief of pulmonary and critical care medicine, and in 2022, he became the CEO of the system.

In these leadership positions, Lateef led Rush’s response to the COVID-19 pandemic, earning both him and the system widespread recognition. Under Lateef’s leadership, Rush University Medical Center’s standing among the nation’s premier academic medical centers has grown significantly, including repeated placement on the U.S. News & World Report Honor Roll, according to the organization. He helped establish national benchmarks for quality and safety through Rush’s leadership in Vizient quality outcomes.

“Omar’s career at Rush exemplifies extraordinary dedication to our mission and our patients,” said William Downe, Chair of the Rush University System for Health Board of Directors. “From his arrival as a fellow more than two decades ago to his service as President and CEO, he has helped shape Rush into the organization it is today.”

Bell, who has served as CEO for 11 years, announced her intent to retire in 2024, beginning the search for CHOP’s new leader. In April 2025, Mitchell joined CHOP and began to work closely alongside Bell, the Board, and their C-suite to ensure a smooth transition.

Mitchell brings over 20 years of healthcare leadership experience, including roles at both Boston Children’s Hospital and Franciscan Children’s Hospital. At Franciscan, he secured approvals and capital to support its 10-year institutional master plan. Prior to this, he worked at McKinsey & Company for 14 years, including seven years as partner.

>Morrison Foerster, a top global law firm, announced the addition of two new partners in the firm's Intellectual Property Litigation Group and Life Sciences + Healthcare divisions. Ching Lee Fukuda and Tom Broughan provide nearly five decades of combined experience in patent and trade secret law regarding medical devices, medtech, life sciences, and technology.

Fukuda previously served as Sidley Austin’s co-leader of global IP litigation and a member of their Global Life Sciences leadership team. She was a partner there for over a decade, winning trial victories before district courts, the Federal Circuit, the International Trade Commission and the Patent Trial and Appeal Board.  Fukuda’s work has been recognized by many legal publications, and she was named Patent Lawyer of the Year by Women in Business Law Americas in 2024.

Broughan also joins Morrison Foerster from Sidley Austin, bringing similar experience in IP litigation in district court. The two of them have worked together extensively, according to Morrison Foerster’s press release.

> The American Medical Association has named Sandra Adamson Fryhofer, M.D., as its next president-elect, a decision that comes as the current holder of that title, Willie Underwood III, M.D., takes the place of outgoing president Bobby Mukkamala, M.D.

Fryhofer is a board-certified internal medicine physician with a full-time practice in Atlanta and an adjust associate professor of medicine role at Emory University School of Medicine. She's been a member of the group's House of Delegates since 1999, elected to its Board of Trustees in 2018 and served as the board's chair from 2022 to 2023. She also previously had leading roles at the American College of Physicians as its president and a member of its Board of Regents.

Fryhofer has been a prominent voice in public health. Alongside serving as the AMA's liaison to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention’s Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (CDC ACIP), she has been a regular medical correspondent and contributor for CNN and Medscape.

“Medicine is at its best when we put patients first and remain guided by science and evidence," Fryhofer said in the organization's announcement. "We have a tremendous opportunity to improve public health and ensure every patient has access to high-quality care. I am honored to serve as president-elect as we continue that work together.”

The AMA's members elect presidents for one-year terms that turnover every June during its Annual Meeting of the House of Delegates. Underwood, a urologic surgeon with expertise in healthcare disparities and policy, was selected in last year's vote and will begin his term this week.

According to the Board, in this process, they “recognized Ben’s strategic capabilities, operational leadership and his experience developing and implementing many of Ridgeview’s key business priorities.” He has held multiple leadership roles at Ridgeview from 2010 onwards, including as chief operating officer, where he coordinated the health system’s ambulatory operations across campuses and locations.

Mike Phelps will remain with Ridgeview through Jan. 2, 2027, to assist with a smooth transition. He emphasizes that “Ben’s character and appreciation for our strong culture — centered on ensuring everyone we care for matters here — will help sustain our commitment to caring for our communities while keeping Ridgeview a strong, healthy organization where staff can invest their valuable time and careers.”

>Anant Vinjamoori, M.D., will serve as the new Chief Medical Officer for the Hims brand, an online comprehensive care platform. With more than a decade of clinical and executive experience, Vinjamoori brings expertise in preventive and longevity care that incorporates diagnostic and therapeutic technologies.

Prior to joining Hims, he founded Next Generation Medicine, which combines medical education with AI technology to assist clinicians with incorporating longevity medicine. >The Melanoma Research Alliance (MRA), a nonprofit funder of melanoma research, announced that beginning on July 1, 2026, Stephanie Kauffmann will serve as the organization's Chief Executive Officer. Joining MRA in 2022 as Chief Operating Officer and President, she oversaw growth in research investment and philanthropic and corporate partnerships.

Kauffman will replace Marc Hulbert, Ph.D., who is leaving MRA to become CEO of the National Kidney Foundation. >Ardent Health President and CEO Marty Bonick "has stepped down to pursue other opportunities," the for-profit health system announced this week. Replacing him immediately is Dave Caspers, who had been serving as chief operating officer.

Bonick helmed the company through a string of challenges, including the COVID-19 pandemic and its 2024 initial public offering. Board Chairman Mark Sotir noted that work in the announcement and said the former head executive had positioned Ardent "for sustained long-term growth and help[ed] build a strong foundation as the Company continues to navigate industry headwinds with a focus on disciplined execution. We wish him all the best in his next chapter."

Caspers, meanwhile, joined Ardent in March 2025 but has spent decades in operational roles. These include senior healthcare leadership positions at Walmart Health, Banner Health and Target. He's spent the past year in the weeds of Ardent's IMPACT (Improve Margins, Performance, Agility and Care Transformation) Program.

"Over the past year, I have had the privilege of working closely with Marty, whose leadership and guidance have been instrumental in laying the solid footing on which Ardent Health now stands," said Caspers, who has also joined Ardent's board. "Today, Ardent Health and our best-in-class team are well-positioned to accelerate our next phase of growth, and I am eager to build on this momentum with a continued focus on delivering high-quality care to the communities we serve.”

>Drew Altman, M.D., founding president and CEO of the influential heath policy research, polling and news nonprofit KFF, will retire from his roles at the end of this year.

He formed the organization in 1990 out of the Henry J. Kaiser Family Foundation, and has since expanded its scope to include the country's largest newsroom focused on health policy, KFF Health News.

“With two small grandkids in Sacramento and Atlanta and another on the way, after what will be almost 37 years of very hands-on leadership since I founded KFF, and with KFF at the apex of its effectiveness, and the opportunity for a perfect transition, the clock in my head is telling me it’s time,” Altman said in this week's announcement.

Following the recommendation of a search committee, KFF has unanimously appointed two in-house leaders to take on Altman's titles: Larry Levitt, currently executive vice president for health policy, as CEO; and Mollyann Brodie, Ph.D., currently executive vice president and chief operating officer, as president.

The pair have a combined six decades of experience at KFF already under their belt, and will take on board positions with their new roles. They will work with Altman to ensure a smooth transition through the end of the year, officially stepping into his shoes in January 2027

>Visory Health, a prescription savings program, has promoted two executives: Alexandra Robertson to chief growth officer, and Brittney Tierce to chief technology officer.

Robertson started as vice president of growth, driving the company's expansion and presence in more than 38,000 pharmacies nationwide. She will now oversee growth strategy, partnership development and program innovation.

"Visory is positioned to redefine what prescription access looks like for underserved populations, making affordability and access the norm, not the exception," Robertson said in a press release shared in advance with Fierce Healthcare.

Meanwhile, Tierce created the tech underlying Visory. She will now drive the tech strategy company-wide, including security, tech and AI innovation.

"Technology should be a bridge, not a barrier," Tierce said in the announcement. "My focus is raising that bar continuously by creating smarter systems, faster delivery and infrastructure that scales access to the communities we serve."

> Healthcare AI company Abridge brought on board a new chief technology officer, San Oo, as it expands its technology into more health systems. Oo joins Abridge after previously holding leadership roles at Slack and Notion, two popular workplace and productivity software tools.

At Notion, Oo set engineering goals and directions, with a strong focus on reliability, developer velocity and agentic engineering, according to an Abridge press release. At Slack, he helped build the engineering organization during the "hypergrowth years" that defined modern workplace collaboration. Earlier, he served as CTO at ShopBack, a unicorn in the Asia-Pacific region. He was also a founding member of two companies that were later acquired: Mumbo Inc., acquired by LinkedIn in 2012, and Astro Technology, acquired by Slack in 2018.

"The platforms San helped build altered the texture of work with thoughtful technology and exceptional craft that facilitate human connection,” Shiv Rao, M.D., CEO and founder of Abridge, said in a statement. “Healthcare is the next industry that needs that kind of care and platform-level rethink, and San is the rare engineering leader who's done it multiple times."

Abridge is rapidly scaling its AI scribe and clinical decision support technology. It is live in more than 270 of the largest health systems in the U.S., including Duke Health, Johns Hopkins Medicine, Sutter Health and Yale New Haven Health. The platform will support more than 100 million patient conversations in 2026.

"I've spent my career building products and platforms that people love to use that are secure and reliable. Now I want to do the same for healthcare, where the stakes are even higher,” Oo said in a statement. “Abridge already sits at the center of the most important moment in healthcare, the conversation between a clinician and a patient. The opportunity now is to extend that intelligence across the entire system."

> WellSpan Health President and CEO Roxanna Gapstur, Ph.D, is calling it a career after more than eight years at the nonprofit system's helm.

Finke is an experienced insurance executive and will be tasked with overseeing and scaling the company's Medicaid and commercial segments. He joins the insurer from Convey Health Solutions, where he was CEO, and has also previously served as president of Aetna at CVS Health.

> AI-powered prior authorization company Humata Health is bringing Liz Fowler, former director of the Center for Medicare and Medicaid Innovation (CMMI), onto its board of directors.

> Jeff Balser, M.D., Ph.D., is retiring from his roles as CEO of Vanderbilt Health and dean of Vanderbilt University School of Medicine at the end of 2026.

The academic system's board of directors has kicked of a national search for a successor who would hold both titles, according to the March 12 announcement.

A 1990 Vanderbilt graduate himself, Balser spent years as a cardiac anesthesiologist and surgical intensivist at Johns Hopkins before returning to Vanderbilt in 1998. He was named chair of the Department of Anesthesiology in 2001, dean of the medical school in 2008 and head of the Vanderbilt University Medical Center in 2009. He has served as dean and in the health system's top executive role for the past 17 years.

The organization has grown from a four-hospital system to an eight-hospital system under his tenure, during which its annual net revenue also grew from around $2 billion to nearly $9 billion, according to the announcement. Part of that time was an effort he led to legally and financially separate the medical center and its system from the university as a standalone nonprofit. The organization's cash reserves also have also grown from 50 days to more than 110, with total cash on hand plus investments now exceeding $3 billion.

> Greg Hoffman, chief financial officer of Providence, will retire in June, the organization announced.

Hoffman has been with the major nonprofit system for over a decade, and served as CFO for the past five years. His tenure in the role is marked by a multi-year financial turnaround that finally brought operations into the black during the quarter ended Sept. 30, 2025. The announcement of his retirement also pointed to his role striking up new strategic partnerships, resetting contracts with commercial payers and heading the implementation of an enterprise resource planning system.

> James Downing, M.D., will be stepping down from his role as president and CEO of St. Jude Children's Research Hospital near the end of 2026, the pediatric research hospital announced.

Under his leadership St. Jude underwent the largest strategic expansion in its history, which included the completion of two separate strategic plans requiring nearly $20 billion of investment and over 2,300 new positions; major expansion to St. Jude's main campus; and greater international collaboration.

>The Department of Health and Human Services announced several leadership changes as the agency looks to advance Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr.'s Make American Healthy Again agenda.

HHS said that Chris Klomp will step into the role of chief counselor at the agency, where he will "oversee all operations of the department." Klomp currently serves as the director of Medicare.

In addition, both Politico and the Washington Post have reported that Jim O'Neill, deputy secretary of HHS and the acting director of the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, will step down from both roles. Sources told the Post that O'Neill will be offered a position as an ambassador.

The Post reported that the shakeup comes amid significant controversy over the shift at HHS in vaccine policy and other hot-button topics. Kennedy is looking to shift talking points to more politically palatable topics, like healthy food, per the Post.

In addition to the new title for Klomp, Kennedy has tapped Kyle Diamantas and Grace Graham to serve as senior counselors for the Food and Drug Administration as well as John Brooks to step in as senior counselor for the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services.

HHS said that each of these three will also continue to hold their current roles in addition to supporting Kennedy's office.

"I am proud to elevate battle-tested, principled leaders onto my immediate team—individuals with the courage and experience to help us move faster and go further as we work to Make America Healthy Again," Kennedy said in the press release.

>The Federation of American Hospitals announced multiple leadership appointments this week.

The organization's new CEO, Charlene MacDonald, took the role on Jan. 1, and on Jan. 14, FAH revealed the names of key members of her team, including:

McBride brings more than 25 years of overall healthcare experience to the COO role, including more than four years at FAH as its leading voice on patient safety, quality and public health. Broder has been at FAH for more than two years, and will take on an expanded role as chief strategy officer, spearheading efforts to support the organization's mission as well as its long-term stability and growth.

Keefe joined FAH in December 2024 and has more than two decades of policy experience, both at the state and federal levels. Tenoever will continue in the general counsel role, and has held similar titles at other trade associations, FAH said.

“Our exceptional new executive team will be integral to the Federation’s success as we write our next chapter,” MacDonald said in the announcement. “We’re at a critical moment for health care coverage and access, and our team’s proven ability to be strategic, nimble, and relentlessly solutions-oriented will allow the Federation to deliver for our members and the millions of patients they serve.”

>Christian Pass was named chief financial officer of Keck Medicine of USC, and will officially begin the new role on Jan. 12.

He comes to the academic health system from Optum, where he was president of provider and payvider enterprise clients. Before that he had several senior financial leadership titles at John Muir Health that culminated in a chief financial officer role.

“Pass has more than 30 years of health care finance leadership experience with a proven history of cultivating high-performing teams and guiding organizations through critical financial and operational transformations,” Rod Hanners, CEO of Keck Medicine, said in a release. “He brings tremendous knowledge and skill to this position that will support the continued growth of the health system.”

>Sarah Ness officially began her new job as president and CEO of PeaceHealth on Jan. 3.

Ness has been with the nine-hospital system for more than 20 years, most recently as its executive vice president and chief administrative officer. She's credited with leading organizational transformations within the system's culture, technology systems and operations.

She takes over for Liz Dunne, who had announced her retirement last year.

>Chip Hubbs, the president CEO of Marysville, Ohio-based Memorial Health, shared plans to retire at the end of this year.

He's held the roles for nearly 22 years, and before that was the CEO of Community Memorial Hospital elsewhere in the state. "In all, Chip has worked for nine different hospitals throughout his career in every facet from groundskeeper and maintenance to intern, fellow, Evening Administrator, Executive Director, and CEO," according to the announcement.

Hubbs plans to work with the health system's board on a long-term strategy and transition plan during his final year in the office.

>The Pharmaceutical Care Management Association, the lobbying group representing pharmacy benefit managers, has named longtime policy expert David Marin as its new CEO, succeeding J.C. Scott.

Marin will officially take the CEO chair on Jan. 20, according to a PCMA announcement. He comes to the organization from drugmaker Viatris, where he was the global head of government affairs, public policy and advocacy. He also previously served as a managing principal at Podesta Group, where he led advocacy efforts for a slew of firms including the Blue Cross Blue Shield Association and Mylan.

He also held key staff roles on the Hill, according to the announcement.

“David Marin is the ideal leader for PCMA at a time of significant change in our industry,” said PCMA Board Chair Adam Kautzner, President of Express Scripts and Evernorth Care Management.

In addition to Marin's hire, PCMA announced that Brendan Buck will serve as the organization's new chief communications officer, also effective on Jan. 20. Buck joins PCMA from public affairs firm Seven Letter.
